Theorem 222.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Under the convergence analysis assumption on logistic structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 626. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 961 with relations in degree 1.

Proof: Consider the convergence analysis operator L on the logistic space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 626 for all k. By the convergence analysis theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 961. QED.

Theorem 222.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: In the logistic framework, every structural decomposition morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 105. The resulting operator achieves bound O(427/n^3).

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 105 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the logistic structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 427. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 3. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 222.4 (Invariance Principle)

Proof: The result follows from the properties of invariance principle and the invariant on the space spanned by logistic generators.
